📖 全文中譯 　　當一項衡量指標變成了目標，它通常就不再是個好的衡量指標。這個觀察由來已久，久到有了自己的名字；也熟悉到常在會議上被引用——然而組織仍然一再對它感到意外。

　　機制很直白。一個數字被選中，是因為它與組織真正在意的某件事相關。舉例來說，平均通話處理時間與效率相關——前提是沒有人正在試圖改善它。一旦人員因為縮短它而得到獎勵，這個相關性就會弱化：電話在問題解決之前就被結束，而來電者隔天又打來一次。呈報的數字改善了，底下的服務卻惡化了。

　　常見的兩種反應，單獨採用都效果不好。第一種是增加更多指標，理由是「同時操弄好幾個數字比操弄一個難」。這確實有幫助，但只到人員發現「其中哪一個指標是真的有人在看」為止。第二種是乾脆放棄衡量，這等於用「沒有圖像」取代「被扭曲的圖像」，而且往往會讓「在會議上最會講話的人」占上風。

　　更有用的做法，是把指標當成「問題」而不是「判決」。數字突然變好，是一個「該問發生了什麼變化」的理由，而不是一個慶祝的場合。申訴件數要與處理時間一起看，因為「一個下降、另一個上升」所提供的資訊，是兩個數字單獨呈現時都給不了的。而最重要的是：要去問那些負責蒐集資料的人，他們認為這些數字是什麼意思——他們通常知道，而且很少被問。

　　這比一面儀表板令人不滿足。它需要判斷，而判斷無法下授給系統；它產生的結論，也比較難用一張投影片就撐住。但另一個選項是：用一組所有相關的人都知道是假的數字來管理——那對任何決定而言都是薄弱的基礎，不論它被呈現得多有自信。
📝 值得抄進單字本的字 measure / target 衡量指標／目標｜correlate with 與…相關｜prematurely 過早地｜deteriorate 惡化｜indicator 指標｜game the numbers 操弄數字｜distorted 被扭曲的｜persuasive 有說服力的｜verdict 判決、定論｜alongside 併同｜informative 有資訊價值的｜delegate 下授｜foundation 基礎
💡 一魚三吃 專業科目：KPI 設計、成果（outcome）vs 產出（output）、資料品質——資訊管理與地方情境題都會考。
法學：第 300 題（裁量怠惰：一律照最高額罰，就是「把判斷下授給表格」）
作文金句：「當一項衡量指標變成了目標，它就不再是個好的衡量指標。」——論「制度與人性」時的第一句就能用。
閱讀 19跨機關資料共享 · 401～405（5 題）
　　Few proposals sound more obviously sensible than allowing government agencies to share the data they already hold. Citizens are asked repeatedly for the same information; agencies duplicate verification work; and inconsistencies between records cause errors that nobody intended. Yet sharing schemes fail far more often than their apparent simplicity would suggest, and the reasons are worth understanding before the next one is designed.

　　The first obstacle is legal rather than technical. Data held for one statutory purpose cannot automatically be used for another; the authority to collect is not the authority to share. Where a scheme proceeds without a clear legal basis, it tends to acquire one retrospectively, which is precisely the sequence that erodes public confidence.

　　The second obstacle is definitional. Two agencies may both record "household income" and mean different things by it: one includes transfers, the other does not; one uses the previous calendar year, the other the previous twelve months. Merged without reconciliation, the two datasets produce figures that are internally consistent and externally wrong. Experienced practitioners spend more time negotiating definitions than moving data, and they are right to.

　　The third obstacle is incentive. An agency that shares data assumes reputational risk if the data proves inaccurate, while the benefits accrue mainly to the recipient. Unless the sharing agency is credited for the improvement, or at least protected from blame for errors it did not create, the rational course is delay. Many schemes described as blocked by privacy concerns are in fact blocked by this asymmetry.

　　None of these obstacles is decisive. Successful schemes tend to share three features: a specific legal authorization rather than a general one; a published data dictionary agreed by all participants; and a governance body with the authority to settle disputes about meaning. What they do not share is enthusiasm at the outset. The schemes that begin with a signing ceremony and no data dictionary are the ones that quietly disappear.
401. What does the author identify as the first obstacle to data sharing?
(A) Insufficient computing capacity
(B) That authority to collect data is not authority to share it
(C) Staff resistance
(D) The cost of software licences
402. What does the author say about schemes that proceed without a clear legal basis?
(A) They usually succeed anyway.
(B) They tend to acquire a legal basis afterwards, which erodes public confidence.
(C) They are always stopped by the courts.
(D) They are the most efficient option.
403. What problem arises when two definitions of "household income" are merged without reconciliation?
(A) The data cannot be stored.
(B) The result is internally consistent but externally wrong.
(C) The datasets become identical.
(D) The figures cannot be calculated.

(C) Privacy law forbids all sharing.
(D) They are not asked.
405. What does the author suggest about schemes that begin with a signing ceremony and no data dictionary?
(A) They are the most successful.
(B) They tend to fade away without achieving much.

(D) They are cheaper to run.

細節題		(B)第 2 段：the authority to collect is not the authority to share——原句照抄
402
細節題		(B)第 2 段：tends to acquire one retrospectively, which is precisely the sequence that erodes public confidence

推論題		(B)末句：are the ones that quietly disappear——「悄悄消失」＝ 沒做出成果就淡掉
📖 全文中譯 　　很少有提案聽起來比「讓政府機關共享它們手上既有的資料」更顯然合理。民眾被反覆索取同樣的資訊；機關重複做著同樣的查驗工作；而紀錄之間的不一致，造成沒有人希望發生的錯誤。然而資料共享計畫失敗的頻率，遠高於它表面上的簡單所暗示的——而這些原因，值得在設計下一個計畫之前先弄清楚。

　　第一個障礙是法律的，而不是技術的。為某一法定目的所保有的資料，不能自動用於另一目的；有權蒐集，不等於有權共享。凡是在缺乏明確法律依據的情況下推進的計畫，往往會事後才去補一個依據——而這正是侵蝕公眾信任的順序。

　　第二個障礙是定義的。兩個機關可能都記錄「家戶所得」，卻各指不同的東西：一個把移轉性收入算進去，另一個沒有；一個用前一個曆年，另一個用前十二個月。未經調校就合併，這兩份資料集會產出「內部一致、對外錯誤」的數字。有經驗的實務工作者，花在協商定義上的時間比花在搬資料上更多——而他們是對的。

　　第三個障礙是誘因。提供資料的機關，若資料事後被證明有誤，要承擔聲譽風險；而好處主要歸於接收方。除非提供機關能因這項改善而獲得肯定，或至少能免於為自己並未造成的錯誤受責，否則理性的做法就是拖延。許多被形容為「因隱私考量而卡住」的計畫，其實是被這種不對稱卡住的。

　　這些障礙都不是決定性的。成功的計畫通常具備三項特徵：一項具體的法律授權（而不是概括授權）；一份經所有參與者同意並公開的資料字典；以及一個有權裁決「定義爭議」的治理機構。它們不具備的，是一開始的熱情。那些以簽約典禮開場、卻沒有資料字典的計畫，正是後來悄悄消失的那一批。
📝 值得抄進單字本的字 duplicate 重複｜inconsistency 不一致｜statutory purpose 法定目的｜retrospectively 事後、追溯地｜erode public confidence 侵蝕公眾信任｜definitional 定義上的｜transfers 移轉性收入｜reconciliation 調校、對帳｜practitioner 實務工作者｜incentive 誘因｜accrue to 歸屬於｜asymmetry 不對稱｜data dictionary 資料字典｜governance body 治理機構
💡 一魚三吃 法學：第 341 題（個資法蒐集要件）、目的拘束原則、「有權蒐集≠有權共享」
專業科目：資料字典、主資料管理（MDM）、資料血緣（lineage）——資料庫與資訊管理共同考點。
申論金句：「內部一致、對外錯誤」與「花在協商定義上的時間比搬資料更多」——兩句都能直接寫進資料治理的申論。
閱讀 20遠距工作與公共服務 · 406～410（5 題）
　　The debate about remote work in the public sector is usually conducted as though there were one question to answer. There are at least three, and they have different answers.

　　The first concerns output. Where work consists of discrete, documented tasks — processing applications, drafting responses, reviewing files — the evidence suggests that location matters little and that measured productivity is stable or slightly higher. This is the easiest case, and it is the one most often cited by advocates.

　　The second concerns learning. New staff acquire much of their competence by overhearing: a colleague's telephone call handled well, a supervisor's reasoning about a difficult case, the informal explanation that follows a formal instruction. None of this appears in a procedure manual, and none of it transmits reliably through scheduled meetings. Organizations that moved entirely to remote work report a specific problem two years later — not lower output, but a cohort of staff who can follow rules and cannot yet exercise judgement.

　　The third concerns equity, and it cuts in two directions at once. Remote work expands access for people whose circumstances make daily commuting difficult, including some disabled workers and many carers. At the same time, it can disadvantage those with unsuitable homes, poor connectivity, or caring duties that make uninterrupted concentration impossible. A policy that treats remote work as a uniform benefit will help some of these groups and quietly penalize others.

　　The practical conclusion is unglamorous but defensible. Decisions should be made at the level of the task rather than the organization, learning should be treated as a scheduled activity rather than a by-product of proximity, and any arrangement should be reviewed after a period long enough for its effects on newer staff to become visible. What should not be done is to settle the matter by asserting that one arrangement is inherently superior. The question is not whether remote work succeeds, but for which task, for whom, and at what cost to the next generation of staff.

📖 全文中譯 　　關於公部門遠距工作的爭論，通常被當成只有一個問題要回答。其實至少有三個，而它們的答案並不相同。

　　第一個關於產出。凡工作是由可切分、有紀錄的任務所組成——審件、擬稿、覆核卷宗——證據顯示地點的影響很小，而所衡量到的生產力持平或略高。這是最容易的情況，也是支持者最常引用的一個。

　　第二個關於學習。新進人員的能力，有很大一部分是「旁聽」來的：同事一通處理得很好的電話、主管對一件疑難案件的推理過程、正式指示之後的那段非正式說明。這些都不會出現在作業手冊裡，也都無法透過排定的會議可靠地傳遞。完全轉為遠距的組織，在兩年後回報了一個特定的問題——不是產出下降，而是出現了一批「會照規則做、但還不會做判斷」的人員。

　　第三個關於公平，而它同時往兩個方向切。遠距工作為那些因處境而難以每日通勤的人擴大了機會，包括部分身心障礙工作者與許多照顧者。但同時，它也可能不利於居家環境不適合、網路連線不佳，或有照顧責任而無法不受干擾地專注的人。一項把遠距工作當成「一律是福利」的政策，會幫助其中一部分人，同時悄悄地懲罰另一部分人。

　　實務上的結論並不華麗，但站得住腳。決定應該在「任務」的層次做，而不是在「組織」的層次做；學習應被當成一項排定的活動，而不是「待在一起」的副產品；而任何安排都應在一段足以讓其對新進人員的影響顯現出來的時間之後，予以檢討。不該做的，是靠斷言「某一種安排本質上更優越」來把問題結案。真正的問題不是「遠距工作行不行」，而是：對哪一種任務、對哪些人、以及以下一代人員的什麼代價為交換。

💡 一魚三吃 法學：equity vs equality ——法學第 203 題（實質平等）與字彙第 270 題是同一個觀念。

作文金句：「不是遠距工作行不行，而是對哪一種任務、對哪些人、以什麼代價。」——這種「拆問題」的寫法，是作文從 55 分拉到 65 分的關鍵手法。
閱讀 21緊急採購與程序正義 · 411～415（5 題）
　　Emergencies create a genuine conflict between two things governments are supposed to do: act quickly, and act accountably. Procurement rules exist to secure value for money and to prevent favouritism, but they take time, and during a crisis the cost of delay is measured in harm rather than money. Most legal systems therefore permit expedited procedures in defined circumstances.

　　The difficulty is not the existence of these exceptions but their operation. Reviews of emergency spending across several countries identify a recurring pattern. The exception is invoked correctly at the outset, when the urgency is real. It then continues to be invoked after the urgency has passed, because the expedited route is faster and no one is eager to be the official who slowed things down. What began as a justified departure becomes a habit, and the habit is difficult to reverse because reverting to normal procedure now looks like an accusation about what came before.

　　A second pattern concerns documentation. Under pressure, contemporaneous records are the first thing to be dropped; they feel like an administrative luxury when people are working late to secure supplies. Yet the absence of records is precisely what converts a defensible decision into an indefensible one. A purchase made at an unfavourable price with a written note explaining why no alternative existed is a hard decision recorded honestly. The same purchase with no note is indistinguishable, after the event, from negligence or worse.

　　Some jurisdictions have responded with a structural device rather than an exhortation. Emergency authorizations are granted for a fixed short period and lapse automatically unless renewed, with each renewal requiring a brief written justification. The mechanism does not slow the initial response, because the first authorization is immediate. What it does is shift the default: continuing the exception requires a decision, whereas previously ending it did.

　　The wider lesson is not that emergencies should be treated as ordinary times. It is that the return to ordinary times should be designed in advance, when it can still be discussed calmly, rather than left to be negotiated later by people with an interest in the answer.

📖 全文中譯 　　緊急狀況會在政府該做的兩件事之間，造成一種真實的衝突：快速行動，以及可被課責地行動。採購規定的存在是為了確保物有所值、並防止偏私，但它們需要時間；而在危機期間，延誤的成本是以「傷害」而不是「金錢」來計算的。因此，多數法制都在特定情形下允許簡化程序。

　　困難之處不在於這些例外的存在，而在於它們的運作方式。多個國家對緊急支出的檢討，都指出一個反覆出現的模式。一開始，這個例外被正確地引用，因為急迫性是真實的。接著，在急迫性已經過去之後，它繼續被引用——因為簡化路徑比較快，而且沒有人想當「那個把事情拖慢的官員」。原本正當的偏離，變成了習慣；而這個習慣很難逆轉，因為此時「回到正常程序」看起來像是在指控先前的做法有問題。

　　第二個模式與紀錄有關。在壓力之下，「當下即時的紀錄」是第一個被放棄的東西；當大家正熬夜搶購物資時，它感覺像是一種行政上的奢侈。然而，正是紀錄的缺席，把一個站得住腳的決定變成一個站不住腳的決定。以不利價格所做的採購，若附有一份書面說明「為何當時別無選擇」，那是一個被誠實記錄下來的艱難決定。同樣一筆採購，若沒有那份說明，在事後就與過失（或更糟的情況）無從區別。

　　有些國家的回應不是勸勉，而是一項結構性設計。緊急授權只給一段固定的短期間，除經展延否則自動失效，而每次展延都須附一份簡短的書面理由。這個機制不會延誤初期應變，因為第一次授權是立即的。它真正做到的，是把「預設值」翻轉過來：現在「延續例外」需要一個決定；而在此之前，是「終止例外」才需要一個決定。

　　更大的一課，不是說緊急狀況應該被當成平常時期看待，而是說：「如何回到平常」應該事先設計好——在還能被冷靜討論的時候設計，而不是留給日後由「對答案本身有利害關係的人」去協商。
📝 值得抄進單字本的字 accountably 可課責地｜value for money 物有所值｜favouritism 偏私｜expedited procedure 簡化／加速程序｜invoke 引用（條款）｜departure 偏離（常規）｜revert to 回復為｜contemporaneous records 當下即時紀錄｜administrative luxury 行政奢侈｜defensible 站得住腳的｜indistinguishable from 與…無從區別｜negligence 過失｜lapse 失效｜renewal 展延｜justification 理由說明｜shift the default 翻轉預設值｜exhortation 勸勉

公文：範例 26（檢討報告）——「當時別無選擇」必須當場寫下來，事後才寫就沒有證明力。
作文金句：「如何回到平常，應該在還能冷靜討論的時候先設計好。」——論「制度與應變」的最佳收尾。
閱讀 22自動化與人的判斷 · 416～420（5 題）
　　Automation rarely replaces human judgement outright. More often it relocates it, and the new location is easy to overlook. A system that screens applications does not remove the need for a decision; it moves the decision earlier, into the rules used for screening, and hides it there. The official who once decided case by case now decides once, in advance, for everyone — usually without realizing that this is what has happened.

　　This relocation has two consequences that deserve attention. The first is that errors become systematic rather than random. A tired official misjudges one case; a badly specified rule misjudges every case of a particular kind, consistently and invisibly, until someone thinks to look. Consistency is normally a virtue in administration, and it remains one — but consistent error is worse than inconsistent error, not better, because it is harder to detect and affects more people.

　　The second consequence concerns the people who operate the system. Where a tool is accurate most of the time, its users gradually stop checking it. This is not laziness; it is a rational response to experience, and it is well documented in aviation and medicine as well as in administration. The practical implication is uncomfortable: a system that is right ninety-five per cent of the time may be more dangerous than one that is right eighty per cent of the time, because the first invites trust and the second does not.

　　What follows is not a case against automation, which does work that could not otherwise be done at all. It is a case for designing the human role deliberately instead of leaving it as a residue. Reviewers need to see the reasons for a recommendation, not only the recommendation. A proportion of cases should be examined independently regardless of what the system says, so that drift is discovered by routine rather than by scandal. And the officials who apply the output should be involved in specifying the rules, because they are the only people who know which situations the rules will meet.

　　The underlying point is old and applies well beyond software. Responsibility cannot be transferred to a procedure. It can only be exercised through one, or evaded behind one.

(A) Eliminates it entirely
(B) Relocates it, often into the screening rules, where it is hidden
(C) Makes it more frequent
(D) Transfers it to the applicant
417. Why does the author regard consistent error as worse than inconsistent error?
(A) It is more expensive to correct.
(B) It is harder to detect and affects more people.
(C) It is unlawful.
(D) It occurs more often.
418. Why might a system that is right 95% of the time be more dangerous than one right 80% of the time?
(A) It costs more to build.
(B) It invites trust, so users stop checking it.
(C) It processes more cases.
(D) It is harder to operate.

(C) It belongs to the system designer alone.
